[1282] The modernisation planned for the communication assets (multirole radio sets, satellite communication and tablets for the Battlefield Management System) of the combined arms motorised capability is valid for Defence as a whole

[1283] FTE CIS personnel are linked to them, among whom 210 FTE are general joint support, without being related to the Land dimension specifically

[1284] They support all capability dimensions and are in charge of territorial support

[1285] In the Land dimension table, these personnel are mentioned separately as the joint CIS capability but the necessary investments have been integrated in the general investments of the combined arms motorised capability (personal weaponry and vehicles) and in the specific investments for communication means

[1286] The CIS support of deployed detachments is structured around standard modules that can be deployed in a joint and multinational framework, where the final user’s interoperability and autonomy are ensured

[1287] The technical complexity is centrally managed by specialised CIS personnel

[1288] The growing needs in connectivity and services for the support of operations require robust strategic and tactical connections based on the use of satellite communications

[1289] The logistic capability of Defence is responsible for the supply of food, fuel, ammunition, spare parts and equipment and assures the sustainability of the deployed capabilities

[1290] It is also in charge of the repairs and maintenance of the land forces’ equipment

[1291] The nature of the deployment and the distance from the national territory require deployable logistic support that can rapidly and in a modular way adapt to the specific needs of each mission

[1292] It must also easily integrate into a multinational framework (Joint Logistic Support Group

[1293] With a view to overall efficiency, logistic support on the national territory will be organised as much as possible in cooperation with civilian partners or through outsourcing, if it is cost-reducing and if the effectiveness of Defence can be guaranteed

[1294] With a view to overall efficiency, logistic support on the national territory will be organised as much as possible in cooperation with civilian partners or through outsourcing, if it is cost-reducing and if the effectiveness of Defence can be guaranteed

[1295] During an operational deployment, a maximum centralisation of the logistic means is strived for in order to limit the number of logistic compounds, means and movements while guaranteeing an optimal logistic support with minimum footprint

[1296] Throughout the duration of this strategic vision, Belgian Defence will be able to catch up in the field of equipping the logistic capability, with armoured vehicles that are adapted to the reality of the theatre of operations, in which there is a permanent threat outside the compounds

[1297] These are armoured protected recovery vehicles, armoured protected evacuation vehicles, armoured trucks for the transport of containers and armoured heavy trucks

[1302] Protected Logistic Vehicles All future logistic vehicles deployed in operations will be armoured

[1303] Possibilities of a European Capability Anchoring Within the land forces, cooperation with neighbouring countries has been well developed in terms of quantity

[1304] With the Netherlands and Luxembourg it even takes place within the structural framework of the Benelux defence cooperation

[1305] Besides, our country also has a structural cooperation with France within the Rapid Reaction Corps in Lille and through our contribution to the Eurocorps

[1306] The Dutch-German cooperation structure and the Benelux defence cooperation have also generated cooperation with Germany for land (sub-)capabilities

[1307] These forms of cooperation are primarily focused on the exchange of doctrine, common training and operational preparation (within the framework of EUBG and NRF

[1308] However, there is considerable potential for further intensification of cooperation with regard to the lines of development “education/training” and “equipment” (acquisition, logistic support and maintenance, configuration management

[1309] Economies of scale in these areas can enhance our efficiency

[1310] Consequently, in accordance with the principles developed in chapter 3, the objective for the land capabilities in general is to reinforce the current forms of cooperation in the short term and to deepen the capability anchoring

[1311] A good example in this context is the common organisation of education and training in indirect fire support and logistics, within the Benelux defence cooperation, for which each of the three countries specialises in different parts of the courses

[1312] In 2016 the first common Belgian-Dutch course for artillery piece commander of the heavy (120mm) mortars was organised in Belgium, led by the Belgian artillery

[1313] This common course is the result of the strengthened cooperation between Belgium and the Netherlands concerning artillery education and training for which a division of roles has been agreed upon in order to ensure a better efficiency and interoperability

[1314] Therefore, based on the capability programmes of this strategic vision, opportunities for cooperation need to be identified and pursued as soon as possible by comparing and aligning the defence planning of our land forces firstly with those of our strategic partner countries

[1315] The orientation of the land forces in our neighbouring countries should also be taken into account, in order to have as many opportunities for common deployment in the future as possible, thus enabling common support of identical systems

[1316] Within the time frame planned for the acquisition of a replacement capability for the MPPV (Dingo) and the AIV (Piranha), from 2025 up to and including 2030, already one programme in our neighbouring countries seems to qualify at the moment, i.e. the French Scorpion programme

[1317] This programme would allow for the replacement of the basic vehicles of the combined arms motorised capability by different types of a common international platform, i.e. a lighter version suitable for troop transport and adaptable to manoeuvre, combat support, and combat service support units, and a median version suitable for reconnaissance and combat missions of manoeuvre battalions

[1318] A possible cooperation with France can offer a major opportunity to provide more efficient support (including training, doctrine, maintenance, logistic support) to our combined arms motorised capability

[1319] The development of the combined arms motorised capability shall therefore, if possible, take place within the broader framework of a long-term strategic cooperation of our land forces with one or more strategic partner countries

[1320] In view of the renewed focus on and configuration of the combined arms motorised capability, it is also important that the participation of the Belgian land forces in any overarching command levels contributes to reinforcing the efficiency of the foreseeable operational engagement

[1321] The development of the combined arms motorised capability shall therefore, if possible, take place within the broader framework of a long-term strategic cooperation of our land forces with one or more strategic partner countries, in which the development lines will be organised in common and our motorised brigade will be integrated in the overarching command structures of this/these strategic partner country(ies

[1322] The Strategic Vision for Defence – II

[1323] Defence has the big advantage that reserves can be integrated into its personnel to provide a specific additional capability during peak periods

[1324] In view of the core tasks “collective defence”, “collective security” and “the protection of Belgian citizens all over the world”, the focus is rather on a specialized Reserve that can make a contribution through specific duties that are not – or not sufficiently – available within Defence itself

[1325] In order to allow Defence to continue to focus on its core tasks while making an important and efficient contribution to the support of internal security during peak periods, it will be examined if the existing non-specialist Reserve can be renewed and reactivated

[1326] When the current policy remains unchanged, this non-specialist Reserve will cease to exist within the next five years due to a lack of young recruits

[1327] The objective would be to organize reserve companies consisting of light infantry

[1328] These reserve troops would be able to use leased commercial vehicles and other material (vehicles, equipment, and armament) already present within Defence

[1329] The reserve companies could be attached to the manoeuvre battalions of the combined arms motorised capability, which would also be responsible for their operational preparation

[1330] Today, only professional military personnel can be deployed for national surveillance missions in case of an increased security threat

[1331] This task should be partially taken over by reserve troops in the future

[1332] They could be led by a centralized territorial staff structure (cf reform of the province commands of chapter 6), integrated into the Defence staff

[1333] Special Operations Forces (SOF) are elite troops that are used as a strategic support capability

[1334] According to NATO doctrine, Special Operations Forces have three basic missions: * special reconnaissance: reconnaissance and surveillance activities as well as special human intelligence operations for gathering strategic and/or operational information; * direct action: limited offensive actions to conquer or destroy specific targets, or to capture prisoners or free hostages; * military assistance: supporting allied forces in the entire spectre of violence, from giving basic training to supporting these troops, in particular through direct actions, or by serving as liaison with air assets or intelligence sources

[1335] The core of the Special Operations Forces capability consists of the Special Forces, the operators of the Special Forces Group

[1336] The operators of the Special Forces have been giving military assistance to the Iraqi goverment troops since

[1337] Whereas this mission primarily focused on giving training to the Iraqi Forces, it has evolved to an assist and advice mission from behind the frontline

[1338] A Special Forces Support Group (SFSG) can assist the Special Forces by providing force protection, as a quick reaction force, as a force for securing the environment during direct actions, for diversion and deception operations, and for “seize and hold” operations

[1339] In addition, the SFSG can execute special operations autonomously, especially “military assistance”-type of operations

[1340] The Special Forces Support Group is essential for reinforcing the capability of the Special Forces, in order to participate in rapid response operations (counter-terrorism, rescue of hostages, protection or evacuation of Belgian citizens

[1341] Furthermore, a SOF capability also consists of joint enablers from all dimensions

[1342] Current and Future Content The future Special Operations Forces will consist of the operators of the Special Forces Group, members of the Special Forces Support Group and specialized air assets

[1343] In addition, some personnel members of the joint enablers will receive specific SOF training

[1344] This includes EOD/C-IED teams, dog teams, a medical support capability and influence support teams

[1345] The Special Forces of the current Special Forces Group constitute the core around which a reinforced SOF capability will be built

[1346] The Special Forces Group will grow to a total of about 225 FTE

[1347] The Special Forces Support Group will be based on the current paracommandos within our Defence and will consist of two Ranger battalions

[1348] The Special Forces Support Group must be able to deploy with the same reactivity as the Special Forces themselves

[1349] Therefore, the Ranger battalions need to be continuously (structurally) organized as a combined arms unit

[1350] They also need to have the same SOF mindset

[1351] For that reason, the SOF competence centre (see section on "Command and Operational Support of Capabilities") will not only include the Benelux parachuting school and the commando training centre

[1352] A new competence centre will be established that will provide specific Special Forces training to the Special Forces Group operators, to the Special Forces Support Group personnel, and to selected personnel of the joint enablers whose mission is to support these operators

[1353] The basic vehicles of the Special Forces and the two Ranger battalions are light motorized vehicles of the RVV (Rapid Reaction Vehicle) and LTTV (Light Troop Transport Vehicle) type

[1354] These LTTVs will be acquired in various versions, adapted to the combat support and combat service support elements integrated in the Ranger battalions

[1355] The acquisition of the RRVs has already been approved by the government, whereas the acquisition of the The core of the Special Operations Forces capability consists of the Special Forces, the operators of the Special Forces Group

[1393] Possibilities of a European Capability Anchor In general, Special Operations Forces (SOF) are a strategic capability

[1394] Europe has an important shortage of this capability, which both NATO and the EU consider to be a priority

[1395] Through a NATO programme Defence will work together with Denmark and the Netherlands to set up an international SOCOM (C-SOCC) which can be deployed in expeditionary operations

[1396] Defence will also continue to provide instructors for the International Special Training Centre (ISTC) in Pfullendorf, Germany which offers specialist training to the Special Forces of NATO member states

[1397] In this centre our instructors work together with their American, Danish, Dutch, German, Greek, Italian, Norwegian, and Turkish colleagues

[1398] Within the Benelux Defence Cooperation, Defence already cooperates with the Dutch Korps Commandotroepen for education, training, operations, acquisition of equipment, and command and control structures

[1399] This cooperation will be intensified to achieve more synergy and thus more efficiency

[1400] The possibility to reinforce the good cooperation with the French, German, and American Special Forces in a structural way will also be examined

[1402] Common training as well as other areas of cooperation, such as air supply, will be further developed and if possible, expanded

[1403] The same kind of internationalisation will be pursued for the commando training centre of Marche-les-Dames, also in Belgium

[1404] The integrated operational preparation of a Belgian amphibious company with Dutch or French Marines, still to be examined, indicates the will to expand in an innovative way the European defence cooperation through capability anchoring, in accordance with the ideas developed in chapter

[1405] The reinforcement of the European amphibious capability is demanded by both the EU and NATO

[1406] This initiative would also add to the European anchoring of our Defence through the existing amphibious partnership between the Netherlands and the United Kingdom, and also reinforces the position of our country within the European Amphibious Initiative (EAI) cooperation

[1407] Through a NATO programme Defence will work together with Denmark and the Netherlands to set up an international SOCOM (C-SOCC) which can be deployed in expeditionary operations

[1422] Reinforcement of the Support to Internal Security and Possible Cooperation at National Level An agreement already exists today allowing for the use of the multirole transport helicopters in a non-structural, specific, and temporary way to assist Civil Security during firefighting operations

[1425] The training costs and operating costs are also reimbursed by the Civil Security

[1441] Military construction contributes to the expeditionary deployment by repairing major road infrastructure, and by building or installing military constructions, especially infrastructure for compounds or for personnel protection

[1442] Furthermore, military construction is of major importance to the reconstruction phase after the main combat actions during an armed conflict have come to an end

[1443] This capability is essential for a quick restoration of public services such as the availability of power, drinking water, road infrastructure, waste removal, telecommunications, and so on

[1444] This is important in order to win and keep the support of the local population against opponents

[1447] These troops provide security when restarting the local political, social, and economic process that should eventually lead to a structural solution of a conflict

[1448] Missions in support of the local population are carried out in close cooperation with the influence capability

[1449] The latter capability is responsible for the civil-military interaction which gives guidance to military construction actions (e.g. by prioritising

[1450] Deployment within the framework of international crisis management in case of disasters and international humanitarian missions is also possible

[1469] CBRN (Chemical-Biological-Radiological-Nuclear) Capability The CBRN capability provides joint support to the mobility and survival of armed forces through the detection of CBRN agents and the decontamination of vehicles, equipment, and personnel

[1470] Current and Future Content Defence will reinforce its CBRN capability, building on its current capabilities

[1471] New CBRN reconnaissance vehicles will be acquired for the HSE teams (Hazardous Site Evaluation) that assess sites which are suspected of being contaminated

[1472] Equipment for forensic sampling and field identification will also be acquired as well as equipment to maintain our home-based reach back capability (DLD86) for the examination of samples taken by SIBCRA87 teams and RDOIT

[1473] Part of the mobile decontamination stations and tents will be replaced

[1474] Specialised CBRN reconnaissance and sampling teams will be able to support the Special Operations Forces capability

[1475] Together with the medical capability, medical CBRN teams will also be trained to decontaminate casualties in a CBRN environment and they will be able to provide specialised transport

[1476] Reinforcement of the Support to Internal Security and Possible Cooperation at National Level Because of the proliferation of CBRN weapons and improvised devices, military CBRN capabilities are more likely to be deployed in case of terrorist acts on the national territory

[1477] In light of a terrorist threat and the potential use of CBRN agents by terrorist groups, advisers, a decontamination capability, specialised CBRN reconnaissance and CBRN forensic sampling teams can be made available to support the Civil Protection and the Federal Police

[1478] Moreover, the CBRN capability can also be deployed in case of disasters (e.g. chemical disasters such as the one in Wetteren in

[1493] Explosive Ordnance Disposal (EOD)/Counter-Improvised Explosive Devices (C-IED) Capability The EOD/C-IED capability provides joint support for the neutralisation of ammunition and the dismantling of explosive devices (whether or not improvised) during the deployment of our contingents

[1494] In addition, this capability also provides support to the neutralisation of ammunition during operational preparation

[1495] This capability works together with the CBRN capability for IEDs equipped with CBRN agents

[1496] Current and Future Content The investments that are required for the expeditionary deployment of the EOD/C-IED capability are closely related to those for the combat engineers and are carried out in common

[1497] Part of the investment budgets for major equipment of the combat engineers (included in the combined arms motorised capability) is intended to be used for the EOD/C-IED capability

[1498] This involves the Route Clearance Packages that are necessary for clearing and destroying improvised explosive devices during expeditionary deployment

[1546] Air-to-air refuelling and strategic air transport are two distinct capabilities

[1547] The aircraft mentioned below are able to perform both capabilities simultaneously

[1548] For that reason, they will be considered together

[1549] Air-to-air refuelling is necessary for the execution of long-duration missions with combat aircraft and for the extension of their range

[1550] This capability also reduces the dependence on the support provided by the host nation in case of expeditionary deployments

[1551] Refueling aircraft are also crucial for the deployment of transport aircraft on long distances and with heavy loads

[1552] Strategic air transport allows for the deployment of troops and loads over strategic distances and within the desired response time

[1553] It ensures the expeditionary autonomy of Defence by ensuring the delivery of logistic and operational support

[1554] In addition to its engagement in core tasks, strategic air transport is a much requested capability for humanitarian operations, often to the advantage of civil actors

[1555] Current and Future Content Currently, Belgian Defence does not own any air-to-air refuelling capability

[1556] To this day, our combat aircraft fleet has never benefited from any support from national refuelling aircraft

[1557] Our fleet has been using the existing (often American) capability within NATO

[1558] The aim is to develop a national air-to-air refuelling capability based on a participation in a multinational pool of refuelling aircraft on the one hand, and on the A400M on the other

[1649] The Surface Combatant Capability (Frigate Helicopters Included

[1650] The surface combatant capability is provided by military vessels from the frigate level on

[1651] A frigate is the smallest platform equipped with sensors and weapons capable of protecting other vessels, both merchant and military (such as amphibious vessels and aircraft carriers

[1652] Frigates are also equipped with self-protection means, namely sensors and weapon systems, in order to safely carry out their mission under combined submarine, surface-, cyber- and aerial threats

[1653] Frigate helicopters, tactical drones and small manned vessels are part of the surface combatant capability

[1654] Frigate helicopters and drones can significantly contribute to the maritime common operational picture and the collection of tactical intelligence

[1655] The helicopters are also forward deployable means for combatting submarines and surface targets

[1656] The small vessels are needed for the boarding team on board the frigate

[1657] Current and Future Content Our navy’s current multipurpose frigates both were originally developed during the Cold war to contribute in the fight against submarine threats

[1658] However, some of the submarine sensors and the embarked Lynx helicopter (sensor and weapon carrier) were not included in the acquisition of the frigates from the Netherlands in

[1659] On the other hand, the deployment capability against asymmetrical threats and the capability for maritime security operations (such as counter-piracy) were reinforced by means of additional sensors

[1660] Today, NATO is asking once more for a reinforced capability against a rising submarine threat at the borders of Europe and elsewhere in the world, a capability that modern anti-submarine frigates can provide

[1661] Both current multipurpose frigates will be replaced by two new multipurpose frigates as from

[1662] However, some investments will already be made for studies from 2017 onwards

[1663] This replacement will take place together with the Netherlands

[1664] The frigates will have a reinforced anti-submarine capability thanks to intermediate and long-range sonars, torpedoes and a maritime helicopter (the existing NH90 NATO Frigate Helicopter (NFH), but fully equipped), which can be engaged in anti-submarine and anti-surface warfare

[1665] They will also have a more important surveillance capability by means of integrated tactical drones

[1666] Destroyers and cruisers can also provide a surface combatant capability

[1667] However, considering their more important investment and operating costs, they are not achievable for our navy within the 2030 timeframe

[1668] The Netherlands, however, have four vessels belonging to the destroyer category: the air defence and command frigates

[1669] A boarding consists in going on board a ship and examine it

[1670] Today, NATO is asking once more for a reinforced capability against a rising submarine threat at the borders of Europe and elsewhere in the world, a capability that modern anti-submarine frigates can provide

[1671] The Strategic Vision for Defence

[1672] Our frigates will, if desired, be deployable in a ballistic missile defence-system, as they will be able to launch missiles capable of engaging ballistic missiles outside the atmosphere

[1673] This will have to be done in cooperation with long-range detection radar systems, for example on board air defence frigates

[1674] This BMD role can be supported through international contributions to the specialised NATO structures for this capability (the BMD coordination cell at the SHAPE in Casteau or the BMD Operations Centre (BMDOC) within the Air Component Commander (ACC) in Ramstein

[1675] In the short term, the capability of the current frigates will be reinforced by integrating tactical drones and frigate helicopters

[1676] The helicopter capability will then be integrated on board according to the operational preparation and the operational deployment of the new frigates

[1677] Two tactical drones will be used as a supporting surveillance capability to improve the maritime common operational picture, surveillance and the collection of tactical intelligence

[1678] The current Alouette III type board helicopters will be decommissioned by 2020 at the latest

[1679] The arrival of the NFH is a significant upgrade of the frigate’s possibilities

[1680] The Alouette III does not have any sensors, while the NFH can be used as an advanced sensor in order to contribute to the common operational picture of the submarine and surface Ballistic Missile Defence systems (BMD) are able to protect deployed troops or a national territory against ballistic missiles

[1681] This is a very complex weapons system, not only based on interceptors but also on sensors and systems for battle management, intelligence gathering and communication, command and control

[1682] NATO is asking for a fair contribution of all partner countries in a BMD system that can protect the population, the infrastructure and the troops of the Member States

[1683] This request was clearly formulated in the new political guidance which is the starting point for the NATO Defence Planning Process

[1684] Concept representation of the future Belgian and Dutch multipurpose frigates

[1685] The aim is to procure identical ships in order to maintain the current close cooperation for support of these ships

[1689] The four NATO frigate helicopters were supposed to replace the three current embarked maritime helicopters (Alouette III), and take over the search and rescue mission (SAR) in the context of internal security

[1690] SAR-helicopters are deployed for medical support and evacuations, mainly in national waters; however they can provide assistance on the entire territory

[1723] The new generation of mine countermeasures vessels are specialised ships for demining missions (including EOD/C-IED) at sea

[1724] The so-called “mother ship” keeps a safe distance from the mine threat and deploys a series of unmanned submarine, surface and flying devices, called the “MCM-toolbox”

[1725] These unmanned devices are used to detect, classify, identify and neutralise explosive devices

[1726] EOD/C-IED clearance divers can also be deployed

[1727] Unlike their predecessors, these vessels will be provided with the necessary self-defense capability in order to face an asymmetrical threat, with an improved endurance and a maritime command capability for mine countermeasures operations

[1728] In addition to being operated from mine countermeasures vessels, some systems also need to be operable from land, in confined and shallow waters (CSW), such as ports and locks for instance

[1729] Current and Future Content Our maritime mine countermeasures capability currently consists of: - six tripartite class mine hunters (CMT) (one of which being a spare); - the command and logistical support ship Godetia – supporting the mine hunters; - systems for clearing very shallow waters (VSW

[1730] The tripartite mine hunters will reach the end of their lifespan by

[1731] Compared with more recent platforms, and considering the technological evolution, current mine hunters are limited in terms of transit speed, duration of mine countermeasures operations, space required for a staff capability and self-protection means

[1732] The Godetia that was built in 1965, is now 51 years old, which is well beyond its envisaged service life of 30 years

[1733] The Strategic Vision for Defence

[1734] The decision was made to purchase six new mine countermeasures vessels that guarantee both sufficient mine countermeasures capabilities deployable in our own waters and the possibility for a concurrent long-term expeditionary deployment

[1735] As a command and logistics platform, the Godetia does not have to be replaced by a separate vessel

[1736] There will be enough staff capability on board the new mine countermeasures vessels through the use of command and control modules

[1737] As is the case now with the Godetia, this will allow to conduct multinational mine countermeasures operations

[1738] The new mine countermeasures vessels will also have a more important logistics support capability

[1739] UUV ROV USV UAV TOWED SONAR Mothership MCM Disposal System A complete overview of the systems of the future maritime mine countermeasures capability

[1740] From the relatively large mother ship, unmanned vehicles are deployed in the air (tactical drones), as well as on and under the water surface (Unmanned Surface Vehicles (USV) and Unmanned Underwater Vehicles (UUV) such as sonars, mine sweeping systems and mine clearing systems

[1741] The decision was made to purchase six new mine countermeasures vessels that guarantee both sufficient mine countermeasures capabilities deployable in our own waters and the possibility for a concurrent long-term expeditionary deployment

[1742] The Strategic Vision for Defence – 29 June

[1743] The new mine countermeasures platforms will be larger in order to meet the need for more autonomy, better self-protection and the use of unmanned modules

[1744] Thanks to their modularity, these vessels will also be deployable in a more multifunctional way for, among other things, maritime security operations

[1745] The larger size and the more important embarkation capability make it possible to take other capabilities on board, such as boarding teams or SOF, which represents an asset for other maritime operations in a (semi) permissive environment, including humanitarian missions

[1746] These vessels will also be used to educate and train personnel, including that of other navies (capability building missions

[1747] In addition to the existing mine countermeasures vessels, the Belgian navy also developed a complementary mine-clearance capability for ports and shallow waters

[1748] These operations involve specific small unmanned vehicles (very shallow water modules), steered by an operator from the quayside or from a ship

[1749] This capability will be maintained and modernised

[1750] A REMUS Unmanned Underwater Vehicle can be deployed for de-mining ports and shallow waters, both nationally and during expeditionary operations

[1751] In the context of internal security, this capability (vessels, divers, as well as land-based teams (mine countermeasures modules for harbours and shallow waters)) is essential for the removal of submarine explosive devices

[1752] It may be a matter of historical ammunition, or IEDs from a criminal or terrorist origin

[1753] Possibilities of a European Capability Anchoring The new mine countermeasures vessels will be jointly developed, purchased and supported with the Dutch Defence

[1754] Our Defence maintains its role as leading party for the mine countermeasures vessels within the Dutch-Belgian naval cooperation

[1755] As such, our Defence remains responsible for the material management

[1756] This requires an accurate sizing of personnel for training, education and material management

[1757] Like for the frigates, the operational command of the mine countermeasures vessels will be binational

[1758] Our Defence will remain in charge of the maintenance of all mine countermeasures vessels of the Admiral Benelux fleet

[1759] The building programme for new Belgian and Dutch mine countermeasures vessels will be carried out on a binational basis at the least

[1760] This programme also has a European dimension as its implementation also takes place within the framework of an international programme, coordinated by the EDA

[1761] In October 2014, six member states of the EDA (Germany, Belgium, Estonia, Norway, the Netherlands, and Sweden) initiated a research project on the future of maritime mine countermeasures capabilities

[1762] These countries, together with Spain, Finland, France, Italy, Poland and Portugal also take part in the EDA ‘unmanned maritime systems programme’

[1763] The programme in question wants to provide maritime mine countermeasures technology between 2015 and 2019, which will also be usable for our new vessels

[1764] These unmanned vehicles are considered as an essential element both by the EDA and the countries participating in this project

[1765] Developing these devices is regarded as a European capability priority by EDA

[1766] These cooperative relationships through the EDA can provide a basis for extending (parts of) the Belgian-Dutch programme, which reinforces more European cooperation on the acquisition, and perhaps at a later stage, the support and development of the new mine countermeasures vessels

[1767] Together with the Netherlands, we will continue to play a leading role in the Euro-Atlantic maritime mine countermeasures niche capability

[1771] It is responsible for education and training of international vessel crews and gives operational advice to decision-making and control organs within NATO

[1772] The Coastal Patrol Capability (Including the Maritime Information Hub) This capability is carried out by coastal patrol vessels (CPV

[1773] Coastal patrol vessels are engaged in military operations in waters under the Nation’s responsibility

[1774] The issue at stake is to ensure Maritime Situational Awareness (MSA), to guide and accompany foreign military vessels, and to make a wide range of specific military security-related interventions, such as the surveillance and protection of critical facilities

[1775] These vessels are primarily designed and equipped to patrol independently in coastal areas

[1776] They feature appropriate seaworthiness and endurance, a small crew and a limited amount of sensors and weapons

[1777] These vessels cannot be deployed as a maritime combat capability due to their specific tasks and the corresponding equipment

[1778] The Belgian-Dutch naval mine warfare school Eguermin ensures the education and training of the MCM specialists of both navies as well as of other NATO navies

[1779] The Strategic Vision for Defence – 29 June 2016 Current and Future Content The coastal patrol capability is carried out by the two recently purchased coastal patrol vessels, directed and supported by a command capability for operations in our national waters

[1780] These coastal patrol vessels can be deployed very rapidly, and one vessel is permanently available

[1781] Support of Internal Security Chapter 2 already mentioned that this capability plays an important support role in the context of internal security for fishery control, the struggle against trafficking (drugs, human trafficking, weapons), the conservation of the environment and support in case of disasters and incidents

[1782] Therefore, personnel belonging to Customs and the Maritime and River Police are integrated in the navy command capability for operations in national waters

[1783] Together, they form the Maritime Information Hub

[1784] Each of the three bodies has its own tasks and databases containing information on the entire Belgian coastal area

[1785] They join their forces within the Maritime Information Hub to gather, analyse and share information

[1786] Interdepartmental cooperation within the Maritime Information Hub might also prove beneficial for fire services, the government’s crisis centre and the LInK in case of emergency and relief operations

[1787] One of our coastal patrol vessels escorts a Russian submarine that is passing through the Belgian Exclusive Economic Zone

[1788] Within the framework of NATO, there are agreements on the shadowing of foreign military vessels

[1789] Possibilities of a European Capability Anchoring With a limited investment, mainly in hardware and software, the Maritime Information Hub can comply with the NATO and EU maritime surveillance standards

[1790] The Maritime Information Hub of Zeebrugge is already integrated in the MARSUR network (maritime surveillance), a prime example of European defence cooperation

[1791] MARSUR (maritime surveillance) is an EDA project launched in 2006 and operational since 2014 in 18 EDA Member States, including Belgium but also countries such as the Netherlands, France, Germany and the United Kingdom

[1792] MARSUR is a network that enables European navies to automatically exchange information (such as vessels positions, headings, identification data, and pictures

[1793] This gives us a common picture of the situation in European seas and information on maritime EU operations

[1794] MARSUR is also a precursor of a broader civil-military project of the European Commission that will provide a common maritime information exchange structure for all maritime security stakeholders of the EU member states and the member states of the European Economic Area (EEA

[1795] There is a structural cooperation with the Dutch and the British counterparts for the Maritime Information Hub since 2013 and with France since

[1796] As the German Maritime Security Centre also wishes to join this cooperation structure, a de facto multilateral maritime security centre consultation among these 5 countries is created Personnel of Customs, the Police and of Defence are in constant interaction within the Maritime Information Hub in Zeebrugge to efficiently ensure the law enforcement and the security in the Belgian waters

[1797] for the North Sea region

[1798] Through structured consultation tangible agreements can be achieved on further cooperation and data sharing

[1799] Logistic Operational Support and Strategic Maritime Transportation via vessels with a logistic support capability such as the Godetia and the Joint Logistic Support Ship Zr

[1800] Ms. Karel Doorman are important to increase the sustainability of maritime task forces in the area of operations

[1801] Logistic support ships may be unable to provide as much fuel as refuelling vessels, but they supply other forms of logistic support such as specialised maintenance, a broader medical support capability and the provision of spare parts, ammunition and food

[1802] The reason of existence of logistic support ships and supply vessels is to make sure that other vessels do not have to interrupt their operational activities to resupply in a port

[1803] Today, our country has a limited logistic support capability via the command and logistic support ship Godetia

[1804] This ship can provide specific support to a fleet of mine countermeasures vessels

[1805] In the new concept for the future mine countermeasures vessels, this support does no longer have to be provided by a specific vessel

[1806] For the multipurpose frigates (Surface Combatant capability), our country is counting on the support of tankers and logistic vessels of partner countries during exercises and operational deployment

[1807] NATO and the EU (EDA) both stress the importance of reinforcing the maritime logistic support capability in order to strengthen the autonomous deployment possibilities

[1808] In the future, Defence wants to contribute to the European maritime logistic support capability by participating in the existing capability within the Admiral Benelux fleet

[1809] The possibility for our navy to supply personnel, operating means and/or a helicopter at short notice for the JSS will therefore be assessed

[1810] Belgian participation in the JSS depends on Dutch authorisation, but the Netherlands is seeking international cooperation to make this vessel fully operational

[1811] The JSS offers other facilities as well, such as a limited amphibious support capability, in order to be able to operate independently of port facilities

[1812] A possible Belgian participation in the JSS would also mean that our Defence would receive, in agreement with its partner, an additional possibility of strategic transport of large volumes, for seabasing operations (the logistical support of troops from the sea), access to a helicopter sea base, and an additional option for humanitarian operations with the deployment of the JSS as a hospital ship

[1813] V. Harbour Protection The harbour protection capability can protect vessels and critical port facilities

[1814] This capability includes an aquatic part to protect a port or vessels against a sea-based threat, with sensors (such as sonars), command and control systems, barriers and the personnel required to operate them

[1815] There is also a land-based part to protect a perimeter, composed of personnel and means, serving amongst others for the Force Protection, EOD/C-IED and engineering works

[1816] Current and Future Content NATO is asking our country (via the NATO Defence Planning) for specific expeditionary modules for harbour protection

[1817] These modules constitute one of the alliance’s capability gaps

[1818] This shortfall is also identified within the EU

[1819] This constitutes a new capability with respect to the aquatic part of the harbour protection capability

[1820] Defence will develop this aquatic part, in order to be able to deploy it on a permanent basis

[1821] The level of ambition does not include any land-based contribution

[1822] This contribution will preferably be made by an international partner, but our own troops could provide this harbour protection capability as well

[1845] I. Defence Staff The Defence staff steers Defence’s capabilities and processes

[1846] It encompasses the staff structures from the Chief of Defence level down to and including the components’ staff level and their direct support

[1847] Greater focus on the staff structures directly connected to the operational deployment should result in flattening the structure

[1848] The new Defence staff will make up about 10% of the personnel of the future Defence (2,545 FTE), which is a reduction of 30% compared to the 3,700 positions planned today

[1849] The scale of this reduction is even more radical when one takes into account that the Defence capability portfolio will be expanded

[1850] The extent to which the FTE of the Defence staff can be reduced is in fact limited, and depends in the Different parts of the Defence staff are directly involved with the operational deployment

[1851] For example, the operations centre of Evere ensures the command and the coordination of the support of our operationally deployed troops

[1852] The new Defence staff will make up about 10% of the personnel of the future Defence (2,545 FTE), which is a reduction of 30% compared to the 3,700 positions planned today

[1853] first place on the number of capabilities a Defence has and only in the second place on the respective size of these capabilities

[1854] The structure of the new Defence staff is one of the implementation projects of the strategic vision

[1855] Although the positions in the Defence staff are not operational deployable positions, Defence staff personnel can be sent to foreign operations as reinforcement, often in international command structures

[1891] Medical Support Medical support is an essential form of combat service support that needs to be adjusted to the level of ambition of the various capability dimensions

[1892] It is organised in accordance with the planned operational deployment for the three core tasks

[1893] However, proper coordination with national (medical) security actors and national health services has to be pursued permanently in order to optimise the supporting role of the military medical elements within internal security and to guarantee maximum access to national operational preparation (training) possibilities for operational deployment

[1894] In general, today the military medical capability (with a little over 1,800 FTE) represents only a small proportion of Defence as a whole

[1895] With 5.4% this capability is relatively smaller than the ones in our neighbouring countries

[1896] The proposed evolution to 1,755 FTE for the overall medical capability in this strategic vision corresponds to a percentage of 7% for a Defence of 25,000 FTE, which is comparable to the current situation in the Netherlands

[1897] This FTE number will be explained in detail below, in accordance with the three big pillars in which the military medical capability is organised: an operational pillar, operational preparation and support

[1898] In addition to the personnel of the operational pillar, a part of the personnel for operational preparation and support can also be deployed in operations

[1899] In the table “command and operational support” the 52 FTE of the medical capability that are part of the Defence staff as well as the 96 FTE manning the Competence Centre Medical are included in the total numbers of the Defence staff and as a part of competence development respectively

[1900] The remaining FTE of the medical capability are those included in “medical support” (1,607 FTE) in the table

[1904] Medical support is an essential form of combat service support that needs to be adjusted to the level of ambition of the various capability dimensions

[1905] It is organised in accordance with the planned operational deployment for the three core tasks

[1906] The Strategic Vision for Defence – 29 June

[1907] The operational Pillar The operational pillar consists of all medical capabilities that contribute directly to the operational deployment of the capabilities of the four dimensions

[1908] They are organised in accordance with the NATO provisions on this matter that are intended to allow for treating casualties as soon and as continuously as possible depending on the severity of their injuries

[1909] Recent NATO operations have made the timings in NATO’s medical doctrine stricter and thus emphasised the importance of a rapid operational medical capability

[1910] As a result, forward and tactical air assets for medical transport (helicopters and STOL aircraft) are gaining importance in this doctrine but they represent a capability gap at the European level

[1911] The military medical capability is in general not sufficiently available within NATO and the EU

[1912] Besides maintaining sufficient medical capability to be able to provide autonomous national medical support to our troops it is important that our Defence can quantitatively contribute sufficient medical capability to the capability objectives of NATO and of the EU within the framework of multilateral burden sharing

[1913] Therefore, the level of our medical capability needs to be in accordance with Defence’s general level of ambition for the four capability dimensions

[1914] In the continuous healthcare chain, the military medical capability does not provide the lowest level of medical care itself

[1915] But it is responsible for the training and operational preparation of each individual serviceman and for those who receive additional medical training outside of the medical capability

[1916] Every serviceman should at least be able to carry out basic medical procedures in order to save lives and avoid irreparable damage before more specialised medical support can take over

[1917] In NATO terminology, the non-specialised basic level of medical care is called Role

[1918] The basic level of the specialised medical capability (thus within the operational pillar of our medical support) are the Role 1 medical facilities that stabilise casualties in operations so that they can be evacuated

[1919] The Role 1 elements also provide medical treatment in a compound during operations, comparable to the care provided by a general practitioner and a nurse (primary healthcare

[1920] The Role 1 facilities are very close to the expeditionary capabilities and should be regarded as an essential combat service support element which should be integrated as much as possible into the combined arms operational preparation and training

[1921] Therefore, Role 1 medical facilities and deployable operational units should as much as possible be set up at the same place

[1922] Operational preparation and support of these Role 1 medical facilities shall continue to be run centrally, for maximum efficiency

[1923] Forward air assets carry out the transport of casualties between the site where the injuries were sustained and a Role 1 medical facility

[1924] Tactical air assets carry out the transport of casualties between different medical facilities in the theatre of operations

[1925] A “Role 1” medical treatment facility is defined by NATO as a medical response capability which is a national responsibility and which provides primary health care, specialised first aid, triage, resuscitation and stabilisation (NATO Standard AJP-4.10 Allied Joint Doctrine for Medical Support, NATO, May

[1926] The military medical capability is in general not sufficiently available within NATO and the EU

[1927] The Strategic Vision for Defence – 29 June

[1928] According to the current NATO doctrine, life-saving surgical procedures are only performed at a higher level of medical operational support, i.e. the Role 2 medical capability

[1929] However, in recent years operational deployment has shown that a limited surgical capability in the first line of the specialised medical support is necessary to increase the possibilities of saving lives and avoiding permanent injuries

[1930] Our Defence wants to play a pioneering role in integrating this capability into the military medical doctrine

[1931] Therefore, Forward Surgical Teams (FST) are to be included in the Role 1 of our military medical capability

[1932] In general, a specific trauma-related surgical capability constitutes a capability gap in the medical capability of most European NATO countries

[1933] The investments planned in medical vehicles of the same type as those of the land forces (a light version of the common international motorised platform and LTTV) are connected with this basic level of operational medical support

[1934] These vehicles are deployed as ground evacuation assets

[1935] Forward and tactical air assets (helicopters and STOL aircraft) can also be deployed at this level or to provide the link to a higher level of operational medical support

[1936] At the moment, Belgian Defence does not have a sufficient capability in forward and tactical medical air evacuation (with helicopters or specialised tactical aircraft

[1937] The budget for SOF Aviation will be reviewed for the possibility to also acquire medical kits for STOL aircraft and/or NH90 helicopters, in order to offer a solution for this national capability gap

[1938] A Belgian contribution to this specific medical evacuation capability is also important since this is a European capability gap and it enhances Defence’s possibilities to support internal security

[1939] However, the primary mission of these helicopters and During operation ATALANTA (2012-13) a serviceman with a gunshot wound is undergoing surgery on board a Belgian frigate

[1940] During counter-piracy operations, the torpedo storage room of our frigates is arranged as a surgery facility for a Forward Surgical Team to be able to quickly administer the appropriate medical care

[1941] STOL aircraft will still be the direct support of the combined arms motorised and Special Operations Forces capabilities

[1942] In addition to Role 1, a national Role 2 capability is indispensable to provide sufficient autonomous operational support and make a sufficient contribution to the multilateral capability burden sharing regarding NATO and EU medical capabilities

[1943] Role 2 medical elements are field hospitals where casualties can receive more specialised care including a full surgical capability with support and follow-up services

[1944] A Role 2 element has a basic structure (Role 2 basic) which, depending on the length and/or nature of an operation, can be expanded with more specialised modules (Role 2 Enhanced), such as a more extensive surgical capability, an intensive care module or a ward

[1945] A “Role 2” medical treatment facility is defined by NATO as an “initial surgical response capability” characterised by its ability to perform surgical interventions in addition to performing reception and triage of casualties, resuscitation and treatment of a shock

[1946] This treatment facility follows the Role 1 capability in which casualties are treated in first instance by a specialised emergency medicine team

[1947] A Role 2 facility ensures in its basic constellation (Role 2 Basic) the surgical capacity, including damage control surgery and surgical procedures for war traumas, to perform life and limb saving medical treatments

[1948] This way the patient can be transported within the specified time limit to the next hospital formation that has more extensive means

[1949] This Role 2 facility can be expanded by additional specialised medical modules to a Role 2 Enhanced which has all medical-technical modules to perform damage control surgery and damage control resuscitation making it possible to stabilise and prepare casualties for strategic (air) evacuation

[1950] The Belgian field hospital (Role 2) in Lebanon that provided medical support to the Belgian and UN troops from 2006 until 2009 at the beginning of the strengthened UN mission UNIFIL

[1951] The Strategic Vision for Defence – 29 June

[1952] The RDOT (Rapid Deployment Outbreak Investigation Team) of the CBRN capability will be able to support the Role 2 in order to reinforce the medical CBRN capability which is also a capability gap in the European NATO countries

[1953] The medical equipment and specialised medical-technical personnel of a Role 2 are employed in the centralised operational preparation structure of the medical capability (see below) when they are not deployed

[1954] The current Belgian medical facilities of Role 1 and 2 levels will soon reach the end of their lifespan and need to be replaced as soon as the budget allows

[1955] To this end, an investment budget of 10 million euros will be provided in 2021 (in the budgetary line for investments in major equipment

[1957] Strategic medical evacuation can be provided using existing patient transport systems that can be installed in the current C-130 transport aircraft and in the future A400M transport aircraft and potentially through an international cooperation programme for a strategic medical evacuation kit for the MRTT pool

[1960] Thus, it is a military hospital responsible for an entire theatre of operations, such as a country or a large region

[2012] An adequate health and well-being policy consisting of prevention, evaluation of the specific medical-military fitness, evaluation of work- and environmentrelated health effects, specific care and expertise is necessary to safeguard the potential of the Defence personnel and to optimise efficiency

[2013] To address these needs Defence develops an integrated health and well-being model through a systematic and methodical approach

[2014] This model guarantees the cohesion of all efforts designed to directly or indirectly improve, preserve or restore the physical and mental health of its personnel

[2015] This model will be realised by providing prevention, occupational medicine, psychosocial support and specific primary and secondary care in an integrated way

[2016] The future of this policy will be part of the analysis regarding a further improvement of efficiency of the military medical support

[2017] Personnel Issues Today the main issue for the medical operational support is finding sufficient medicaltechnical personnel to man the medical capabilities

[2018] This will continue to be a challenge

[2019] This is the case for all medical-technical personnel, such as nurses and doctors

[2021] At this moment for instance, about 70 medical-technical officers are in training

[2022] Adapted regulations will contribute to the job satisfaction of these servicemen, while Defence can count on a higher availability and the development and maintenance of important know-how for the armed forces

[2023] Another way to solve this issue would be strengthening the efforts to attract more doctors and nurses from civilian hospitals to the specialised reserve

[2077] Support Air Various structures support both the territorial and the expeditionary deployment of the Defence air assets

[2078] The Meteorological Wing meets the needs of Defence with regard to meteorological information

[2079] It has an important territorial task for the benefit of the military air assets of Defence and those of our partner countries

[2080] The Meteorological Wing is also essential for the preparation of an operational deployment, as it provides information needed to take into consideration the climatic conditions in the engagement area during the planned engagement period

[2081] Meteorological Wing detachments can be sent in operation and in support of the operational centres

[2082] The Meteorological Wing also supports the national civilian meteorological capability

[2083] The military flight safety (Aviation Safety Directorate) mainly has a territorial task

[2084] It issues and checks the measures taken to prevent air accidents

[2085] In case of an aviation accident involving a Belgian military aircraft or – if requested – any aviation accident in our airspace, this directorate is in charge of the investigation and analysis with regard to flight safety

[2086] During operation Guardian Falcon in Afghanistan, the Meteo Wing was responsible for the support of the operational deployment of the F-16s by gathering meteorological data

[2088] The Air Traffic Control Centre (ATCC) ensures the military air traffic control and is essential for the proper coordination between military and civilian air traffic in our national airspace, which is one of the densest in the world

[2089] As indicated above, the Control and Reporting Centre (CRC) carries out the air combat control

[2090] The missions of the CRC consist in monitoring the national (and in the future Benelux) and international airspaces within the framework of the NATO collective defence

[2091] The CRC is thus part of a chain of other NATO CRCs under the supervision of a NATO command structure

[2092] The CRC guides the combat aircraft that carry out Quick Reaction Alert missions

[2093] At the same time, these aircraft and the CRC also support internal security

[2094] In order to support the expeditionary operations of Belgian Defence’s air assets an air controller of the ATCC is always included as a liaison officer in order to ensure the integration of our aircraft on the deployed base

[2095] During operations the CRC air combat controllers can be integrated into an international command structure

[2096] They can also act as liaison officer in the national Red Card Holder Team that is deployed in the Air Operations Centre to make sure that the national interests and the imposed restrictions (caveats) are observed

[2097] At the moment both the ATCC and the CRC are under review to determine how their duties can be carried out more efficiently through closer national and international cooperation

[2098] As indicated above, the Air Information Hub (Lucht Informatie Kruispunt - LInK) will be integrated into the CRC

[2175] Defence Attachés and Military Advisers A defence attaché is a serviceman or -woman who represents the Minister of Defence and the Chief of Defence in the countries for which he or she is accredited

[2176] A defence attaché has responsibilities in diplomatic and military matters as well as in the field of intelligence

[2177] The defence attaché is the Belgian military representative in the military diplomatic environment, and plays a central role in defence diplomacy

[2178] The defence attaché can assist diplomats in diplomatic negotiations or other matters with important military operational and military technical aspects by using the professional experience and knowledge gained during his or her military career

[2179] The defence attaché also plays an important military operational role in ensuring the local procedures for the core task “protection of Belgian citizens all over the world”

[2180] In order to fulfil his military mission the defence attaché needs to maintain all necessary contacts with the local (military) authorities and with the international (military) representation in order to obtain a correct and up-to-date strategic overview of the country and the (military) actors involved

[2181] The defence attaché actively keeps up with the (military) developments, the security risks, and the opportunities in his region, in particular within the framework of possible, current or planned bilateral and/or multilateral activities and operations

[2182] The role of the defence attaché as an essential link in the intelligence apparatus of our country has already been mentioned above

[2183] Within the applicable legal framework he or she informs the Minister of Defence, the CHOD and the Defence Staff about the military and defence policy of the countries of accreditation and about their armed forces

[2184] Today Defence has a global network of 19 defence attachés and 2 military advisers, as well as staff personnel (68 FTE in total

[2185] Defence attachés do not necessarily reside in the country for which they are accredited

[2186] Whether they reside in their country of accreditation or not does not have any influence on their role and missions

[2187] Many defence 190 The Strategic Vision for Defence – 29 June 2016 attachés abroad have an accreditation for several countries in the same region, and the defence attachés for the European countries work from the Defence staff in Belgium

[2188] In total, the 19 defence attachés are currently accredited for about 76 countries

[2189] The military advisers are members of the Belgian delegations at the United Nations in New York and at the Organisation for Security and Co-operation in Europe in Vienna

[2190] Some defence attachés are also military advisers for regional organisations

[2191] The geographic distribution and the accreditations are the result of a recurrent analysis at the Defence level that considers not only the geopolitical security environment but also our bilateral and/or multilateral relations

[2192] The geographic distribution is also coordinated with the departments of Foreign Affairs and Development Cooperation in order to reinforce a national comprehensive approach with regard to security policy

[2193] By 2030 the number of FTE for defence attachés and military advisers (and their support personnel) will be reduced to 50 FTE

[2194] This reduction will primarily be achieved by a further rationalisation of accreditations of defence attachés and by a more common approach of the national military representation with strategic partner countries

[2195] Today an initiative has already been taken at the Benelux level to issue complementary accreditations to defence attachés who would then work for both Belgium and the Netherlands
